inducing an isomorphism of triples
T_{\psi}^{\prime\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}}(P,\theta,Q)\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}}(P,\theta,Q)
for any triple
(P,\theta,Q)
representing an element of
\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A},A)
.

Claim (
2
) also follows from the straightforward diagram chasing.
∎

For
C^{\bullet}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A})
, a
trivialisation
over
E
(or an
E
-trivialisation
) means an
A
-linear isomorphism

(4.7)

h\colon\bigoplus_{i\text{ even}}\Hrm^{i}(E\otimes_{R}C^{\bullet})\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\bigoplus_{i\text{ odd}}\Hrm^{i}(E\otimes_{R}C^{\bullet}).

By semi-simplicity of
A
, one can naturally extend
h
to an isomorphism
\bigoplus_{i\text{ even}}E\otimes_{R}C^{i}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\bigoplus_{i\text{ odd}}E\otimes_{R}C^{i}
, also denoted by
h
.

Definition 4.8
.

Given a perfect
\mathfrak{A}
-complex with
E
-trivialisation
(C^{\bullet},h)
, we define its
Euler characteristic

\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},h)\in\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A},A)
as follows:

\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},h)\coloneqq\left[\big(\bigoplus_{i\text{ even}}C^{i}\big),h,\big(\bigoplus_{i\text{ odd}}C^{i}\big)\right].

Immediately,
\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},h)
only depends on the isomorphism class of
C^{\bullet}
in
\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A})
.

If
C^{\bullet}
is a perfect
\mathfrak{A}
-complex such that
E\otimes_{R}C^{\bullet}
is acyclic, then we let
\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},0)
denote the Euler characteristic with respect to the
unique

E
-trivialisation on
C^{\bullet}
(i.e., the zero map between the zero modules).

The formation of
\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(-)
is functorial with respect to
\rho^{\psi}
(
4.5
); indeed, given a perfect
\mathfrak{A}
-complex with
E
-trivialisation
(C^{\bullet},h)
, we have

(4.9)

\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},h)\big)=\chi_{R^{\prime},E^{\prime}}([C^{\bullet}]_{\psi},[h]_{\psi}),

where
[h]_{\psi}
is the
E^{\prime}
-trivialisation induced on
[C^{\bullet}]_{\psi}\coloneqq T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}}C^{\bullet}
via
h
.

Example 4.10
.

If
\mathfrak{A}
is a DVR with normalised valuation
v_{A}
, then let
v_{A}
also denote the following isomorphism

v_{A}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.45834pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A},A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.83334pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\text{sending }[\mathfrak{A},a,\mathfrak{A}]\mapsto v_{A}(a).

In that case, for any
C^{\bullet}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A})
with all
\Hrm^{i}(C^{\bullet})
torsion we have

v_{A}\left(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet},0)\right)=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i+1}\lth_{\mathfrak{A}}\big(\Hrm^{i}(C^{\bullet})\big).

Now, let
\mathfrak{p}
be a non-zero
principal
prime ideal of
R
, and write
k\coloneqq R/\mathfrak{p}
.
Given
C^{\bullet}_{k}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})
, we abusively write

\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\coloneqq\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{A}},0)

where
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{A}}
is a perfect
\mathfrak{A}
-complex quasi-isomorphic to
C^{\bullet}_{k}
. (Note that
E\otimes_{R}C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{A}}
is clearly acyclic.) In this case, we have another notion of Euler characteristic; namely,
\chi_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(C^{\bullet}_{k})\in\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})
, which can be related to
\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)
as follows. Define a homomorphism

\jmath_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.51393pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A},A)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

by sending a finitely generated projective
\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}
-module
P_{k}
, viewed also as a complex concentrated in degree
0
, to

\jmath_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(P_{k})\coloneqq\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(P_{k},0)=[\widetilde{P},\varpi^{-1},\widetilde{P}],

where
\widetilde{P}
is a projective
\mathfrak{A}
-module lifting
P_{k}
and
\varpi
is a generator of
\mathfrak{p}
; indeed,
P_{k}
is quasi-isomorphic to a two-term complex
[\widetilde{P}\xrightarrow{\varpi}\widetilde{P}]
concentrated in degree
[-1,0]
, so its Euler characteristic is as above. One can show (by straightforward computation) that this extends to any
C^{\bullet}_{k}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})
; that is,

\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)=\jmath_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(C^{\bullet}_{k})\big).

Furthermore, given
\psi\colon\mathfrak{A}\to\End_{R^{\prime}}(T_{\psi})
as before such that
R^{\prime}
is a DVR whose maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
contains
\mathfrak{p}
, we also have

(4.11)

\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\big)=\chi_{R^{\prime},E^{\prime}}(T^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathfrak{A}}C_{k}^{\bullet},0)=\jmath_{R^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\big(\chi_{R^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}(T_{\psi}^{\ast}/\mathfrak{p}T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}C_{k}^{\bullet})\big).

Lemma 4.12
.

In the above setting, choose an algebraic closure
\bar{k}
of
k
and a
k
-embedding
R^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
. Set
\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\coloneqq T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{R^{\prime}}\bar{k}
and define

\mrm_{\psi}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 24.51393pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.11095pt\raise 7.2721pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.68321pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{T}_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.51393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.61116pt\raise 7.06387pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.79723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\dim_{\bar{k}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 109.36119pt\raise-6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 126.98619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 126.98619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Then for any
C^{\bullet}_{k}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A})
we have

v_{E^{\prime}}\left(\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\big)\right)=-e_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\mrm_{\psi}\left(\chi_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(C^{\bullet}_{k})\right),

where
v_{E^{\prime}}(-)
is the normalised valuation on
E^{\prime}\coloneqq\Frac R^{\prime}
and
e_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{p}}
is the ramification index.

Proof.

By Example
4.10
and (
4.11
), we have

\displaystyle v_{E^{\prime}}\left(\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\big)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i+1}\lth_{R^{\prime}}\left(\Hrm^{i}\big(T_{\psi}^{\ast}/\mathfrak{p}T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}C^{\bullet}_{k}\big)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i+1}\lth_{R^{\prime}}\left(T_{\psi}^{\ast}/\mathfrak{p}T_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}C^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=e_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\sum_{i}(-1)^{i+1}\dim_{\bar{k}}\left(\overline{T}_{\psi}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}C^{i}\right)=-e_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\mrm_{\psi}\left(\chi_{\mathfrak{A}/\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{A}}(C^{\bullet}_{k})\right),

as desired.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

In Lemma
4.12
, the normalised valuation of
\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\big)
changes if we replace
T_{\psi}
by the scalar extension under a finite ramified ring extension, but the formation of the fractional ideal
\mathfrak{a}
whose normalised valuation equals that of
\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi_{\mathfrak{A},E}(C^{\bullet}_{k},0)\big)
commutes with any finite scalar ring extensions.

Now consider a
G
-cover
\pi\colon X_{L}\to X
and a
G
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
in
X_{L}
that satisfies the conditions in Theorem
3.2
. We also assume that the constant field
k
of
X
is
finite
with characteristic
p
. For simplicity we write

(4.14)

\chi^{G}_{p}(\mathcal{E})\coloneqq\chi_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma(X_{L},\mathcal{E})^{\vee},0)\in\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}G),

which makes sense as
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(X_{L},\mathcal{E})^{\vee}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathbb{F}_{p}G)
. Here,
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(X_{L},\mathcal{E})^{\vee}
denotes the Pontryagin dual (or equivalently, the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-linear dual) with the contragredient
G
-action. To motivate this choice, see Theorem
6.14
.
For
\ell\neq p
, we set
\chi^{G}_{\ell}(\mathcal{E})=0
in
\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
.

Let
E^{\prime}
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with valuation ring
R^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}
and residue field
k^{\prime}
. Pick a
G
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}
-lattice
T_{\psi}
in a finitely generated
E^{\prime}G
-module
V_{\psi}
, so we get a map
\psi\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}G\to\End_{R^{\prime}}(T_{\psi})
. We now apply Lemma
4.12
to
\mathfrak{A}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}G
and
\mathfrak{p}=(p)
to obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
\pi\colon X_{L}\to X
be a
G
-cover that is weakly ramified everywhere. For each
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}
, choose
C_{w}\subseteq I_{w}
so that we have
I_{w}=P_{w}\rtimes C_{w}
(
cf.
Lemma
2.7
). We write
\theta_{C_{w}}\coloneqq\theta_{w}|_{C_{w}}
.

Let
k
be the finite constant field of
X
, and fix a
k
-embedding
k_{w}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
for each
w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}
. Finally, fix an embedding
k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
and set
\overline{T}_{\psi}\coloneqq\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}T_{\psi}
.

(1)

For any
w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}/(|C_{w}|)
, we have

\mrm_{\psi,w}(j)\coloneqq\mrm_{\psi}\big(\Ind_{I_{w}}^{G}M_{w}(j)\big)=\sum_{a=0}^{[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]-1}\dim_{\bar{k}}\big(\overline{T}_{\psi}[\theta_{C_{w}}^{jp^{a}}]\big),

Here,
\overline{T}_{\psi}[\theta_{C_{w}}^{s}]
for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
is the maximal subspace of
\overline{T}_{\psi}
where
C_{w}
acts via
\theta_{C_{w}}^{s}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle
X_{L}
that satisfies the condition (
3.2a
) for any
w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{wild}}
.
Then we have

-v_{E^{\prime}}\left(\rho^{\psi}\big(\chi^{G}_{p}(\mathcal{E})\big)\right)=v_{E^{\prime}}(p)\cdot\bigg[[k:\mathbb{F}_{p}]\cdot\dim_{\bar{k}}\Big(\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{k}\chi_{k}\big(\mathcal{E}^{G}\big)\Big)+\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)\bigg]

where

\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)\coloneqq\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\rk\mathcal{E}}\left(-|P_{w}|\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{|C_{w}|-1}j\cdot\mrm_{\psi,w}(-j)+|I_{w}|\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{l_{w,i}}\mrm_{\psi,w}(j)\right).

Here,
\mrm_{\psi,w}(j)
is defined in (
1
), and
l_{w,i}
’s are as in (
3.2b
).

Proof.

By transitivity of inductions, we have
\Ind_{I_{w}}^{G}M_{w}(j)\cong\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}\big((\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}^{j+1})|_{C_{w}}\big)
;
cf.
Lemma
2.10
. Since

\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}(\theta_{C_{w}}^{j})
is a projective
\bar{k}G
-module,
8
8

8

One can show projectivity by realising
\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}(\theta_{C_{w}}^{j})
as a direct summand of
\Ind_{1}^{G}(k_{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi})
.
we have

(4.16)

\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}G}\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}\big((\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}^{j+1})|_{C_{w}}\big)\cong\Big(\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}\big((\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}^{j+1})|_{C_{w}}\big)\Big)^{G}\\
\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}G}\Big(\overline{T}_{\psi},\,\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Ind_{C_{w}}^{G}\big((\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}^{j+1})|_{C_{w}}\big)\Big)\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}[C_{w}]}\big(\overline{T}_{\psi},\,\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}^{j+1})\big),

where the last isomorphism is the Frobenius reciprocity. To conclude, observe that

\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j+1})\cong\bigoplus_{i=0}^{[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]-1}\bar{k}\otimes_{\Fr_{p}^{i},k_{w}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j+1}),

where
\Fr_{p}
is the
p
th power map and
C_{w}
acts on
\bar{k}\otimes_{\Fr_{p}^{i},k_{w}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j}/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{j+1})
via
\theta_{w}^{jp^{i}}
. In particular, the last term in (
4.16
) is the direct sum of the multiplicity spaces for
\theta_{w}^{jp^{i}}
, and thus the displayed equation in claim (
1
) follows.

For any
k
-vector space
V
, we have

\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}G}\Ind_{1}^{G}V\cong\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}V\cong(\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{k}V)^{\oplus[k:\mathbb{F}_{p}]}.

Note also that
M_{w}(j)^{\vee}\cong M_{w}(-j)
, where
M_{w}(j)^{\vee}
is the Pontryagin dual with contragredient
G_{w}
-action.
Claim (
2
) now follows from Theorem
3.2
(
2
) and Lemma
4.12
.
∎

We conclude the section with some remarks on Corollary
4.15
.

Remark 4.17
.

In Corollary
4.15
(
1
), we have
\overline{T}_{\psi}[\theta_{C_{w}}^{s}]\cong\overline{T}^{\mathrm{ss}}_{\psi}[\theta_{C_{w}}^{s}]
as
k_{w}[C_{w}]
is semi-simple, which shows that
\mrm_{\psi,w}(j)
depends only on
j\in\mathbb{Z}/(|C_{w}|)
and
V_{\psi}
, not on the choice of
T_{\psi}\subset V_{\psi}
. In particular, the formula in Corollary
4.15
(
2
) is independent of the choice of
T_{\psi}\subset V_{\psi}
.

Remark 4.18
.

The right hand side of the formula in Corollary
4.15
(
2
) can be divided into two parts – the first terms involves
\deg\psi\coloneqq\dim_{E^{\prime}}V_{\psi}
and the Euler characteristic of
\mathcal{E}^{G}
, and the second term
\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)
measures the “local ramification” of
\overline{T}_{\psi}^{\mathrm{ss}}
and
\mathcal{E}
(that is,
\overline{T}_{\psi}^{\mathrm{ss}}|_{I_{w}}
and
l_{w,i}
’s for any
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}
).

Let us now consider the special case of
\mathcal{E}=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(-Z_{L})
where
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle on
X
and
Z_{L}=\pi^{-1}(Z)
for some closed subset
Z\subset|X|
containing the ramification locus for
\pi
.
Then as in (
3.6b
), the formula in Corollary
4.15
(
2
) can be made more explicit using the following formula:

\displaystyle\dim_{\bar{k}}\Big(\overline{T}^{\ast}_{\psi}\otimes_{k}\chi_{k}\big(\mathcal{E}^{G}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=(\deg\psi)\cdot\Big((\rk\mathcal{F})\cdot\big(1-\gen_{K}-\deg(Z)\big)+\deg(\mathcal{F})\Big);

\displaystyle\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rk\mathcal{E}}{|G|}\sum_{w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}}\sum_{j=1}^{|I_{w}/P_{w}|-1}\sum_{a=0}^{[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]-1}j|P_{w}|\cdot\dim_{\bar{k}}\big(\overline{T}_{\psi}[\theta_{C_{w}}^{jp^{a}}]\big),

where
\deg(Z)=\sum_{v\in Z}[k_{v}:k]
.

Remark 4.19
.

Let us make
\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)
more explicit in some special cases. Firstly, if we have
I_{w}=P_{w}
for all
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}
(e.g., if
G
is a
p
-group or
\pi
is étale), then we have
\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)=0
for any
\psi
and
\mathcal{E}
(
cf.
Remark
3.4
).

Now, suppose that
\pi
is tame everywhere and
\deg\psi=1
. We also specialise to the case when
\mathcal{E}=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(-Z_{L})
as in Remark
4.18
. For any
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}
, let
d_{w}
denote the smallest positive integer such that
p^{d_{w}}\equiv 1\bmod{|I_{w}|}
. (Note that
d_{w}
divides
[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]
.) For each
a=0,\cdots,d_{w}-1
, let
j^{(a)}_{\psi,w}
denote the integer in
\{0,\cdots,|I_{w}|-1\}
such that
I_{w}
acts on
\overline{T}_{\psi}|_{I_{w}}
via
\theta_{w}^{j^{(a)}_{\psi,w}\cdot p^{a}}
. Then we have

(4.19a)

\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)=\frac{\rk\mathcal{E}}{|G|}\sum_{w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}}\sum_{a=0}^{d_{w}-1}j_{\psi,w}^{(a)}\cdot\frac{[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]}{d_{w}}.

If furthermore
|I_{w}|
divides
p-1
for any
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}
(which follows if
|G|
divides
p-1
), then setting
j_{\psi,w}=j_{\psi,w}^{(0)}
we obtain

(4.19b)

\ra_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)=\frac{\rk\mathcal{E}}{|G|}\sum_{w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{ram}}}j_{\psi,w}\cdot[k_{w}:\mathbb{F}_{p}].

5.
Review of Néron models and base change

We use the setting of §
3
. Fix an abelian variety
A
over
K
, and let
\mathcal{A}
denote the Néron model of
A
over
X
. For the pull back
A_{L}
of
A
over
L
, we let
\mathcal{A}_{L}
denote the Néron model, and write
\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{X}X_{L}
. The connected Néron models are denoted as
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}
, etc. Let
A^{t}
denote the dual abelian variety of
A
, and similarly define
\mathcal{A}^{t}
,
\mathcal{A}^{t,\circ}
, etc. We maintain this setting for the rest of the paper.

By the Néron mapping property, the
G
-action on
X_{L}
lifts to a
G
-action on
\mathcal{A}_{L}
, and we get a natural
G
-equivariant homomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}}\to\mathcal{A}_{L}
extending the identity map on the generic fibre. Furthermore, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
U^{\prime}\subset X
be the
maximal
open subscheme such that the natural map
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{X_{L}}|_{U^{\prime}_{L}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}|_{U^{\prime}_{L}}
is an isomorphism, where
U^{\prime}_{L}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(U^{\prime})
.

(1)

The cokernel of the natural inclusion

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}})\cong\pi^{\ast}\Lie(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})}

is supported exactly on
X_{L}\setminus U_{L}^{\prime}
.

(2)

A closed point
v\in|X|
lies in
U^{\prime}
if either
L/K
is unramified at
v
or
A
has semistable reduction at
v
.

(3)

Suppose furthermore that
L/K
is
tamely ramified
at all places in
X^{\prime}\setminus U^{\prime}
. Then the natural map
\Lie(\mathcal{A})\to\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})^{G}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Fix a place
v\in|X|
, and choose a place
w\in|X_{L}|
over
v
. We set

\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{X}\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v},\quad\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}_{L}\times_{X_{L}}\Spec\mathcal{O}_{w},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{X}\Spec\mathcal{O}_{w}.

We similarly define
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}^{\circ}
, etc. By standard properties of Néron models, we have
v\in U^{\prime}
if and only if the natural map
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
is an isomorphism, and
U^{\prime}
contains the unramified locus for
L/K
;
cf.
§1.2, Proposition 4 and §7.2, Corollary 2 in
[
BLR90
]
. If
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}
is a semi-abelian scheme, then
v\in U^{\prime}
by
[
BLR90
, §7.4, Corollary 4]
). This proves (
2
).

If
L_{w}/K_{v}
is tamely ramified, then by
[
Edi92
, Theorem 4.2]
we have a natural isomorphism

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\left(\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}\right)^{G_{w}}}

of group schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{v}
, where
\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
denotes the Weil restriction of scalars. Since the Lie algebra of
\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
coincides with
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathcal{O}_{w})
viewed as an
\mathcal{O}_{v}
-module, it follows that the natural map
\Lie(\mathcal{A})\to\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})^{G}
induces an isomorphism on the completed stalks at all
tame
places
v\in|X|
. Since this map induces an isomorphism on the restriction to
U^{\prime}
by (
2
), we obtain (
3
) by the standard descent argument.

To prove (
1
), we need to show that the natural map
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
is isomorphic if and only if
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}})(\mathcal{O}_{w})\hookrightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathcal{O}_{w})
is isomorphic. The “only if” direction is clear, so suppose that we have
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}})(\mathcal{O}_{w})\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathcal{O}_{w})
. Then the natural map
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
is étale by smoothness of the source and the target (
cf.

[
BLR90
, §2.2, Corollary 10]
), so it is an open immersion by Zariski’s main theorem (
cf.

[
BLR90
, §2.3, Theorem 2
′
]
). Since all the fibres of
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,w}
over
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{w}
is connected, the desired claim now follows.
∎

Set
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\coloneqq\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
, and denote its special fibre by
\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{L,k_{w}}
be the special fibres of
\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
. Then have a natural
G_{w}
-equivariant surjective map

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Res_{k_{w}/k_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,k_{w}}}

with smooth connected unipotent kernel denoted by
\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}
; indeed, this can be seen by realising
\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}
as the Weil restriction of scalars for a nilpotent thickening
\mathcal{O}_{w}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}k_{v}\twoheadrightarrow k_{w}
;
cf.

[
Edi92
, §5.1]
or
[
CGP15
, Proposition A.5.12]
.

We retain the setting that
X
is defined over a
perfect
field
k
of characteristic
p>0
, so
k_{v}
is perfect as well. Then
\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}
is a vector group over
k_{v}
by
[
CGP15
, Corollary B.2.7]
; i.e., it is a direct product of copies of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

.

is tame then the short exact sequence (

) induces the following short exact sequence

which induce the following isomorphism

(5.3b)

\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{w})\big)^{G_{w}}\cong\coker\left(\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathfrak{m}_{w}/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{|I_{w}|})\big)^{G_{w}}\hookrightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v})\right).

Moreover,
\big(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}\big)^{G_{w}}
is a
vector group
over
k_{v}
and the sequence (
5.3a
) remains exact on
k_{v}
-points.

Proof.

Suppose that we know the sequence (
5.3a
) is exact and that
\big(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}\big)^{G_{w}}
is a vector group. Then the isomorphism (
5.3b
) is a direct consequence of the short exact sequence of Lie algebras induced from (
5.3a
), and the sequence (
5.3a
) remains exact on
k_{v}
-points since
\Hrm^{1}(k_{v},(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{w}})^{G_{w}})
is trivial by the Hilbert normal basis theorem.

It remains to show that the sequence (
5.3a
) is exact and that
\big(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}\big)^{G_{w}}
is a vector group, both of which can be checked after base change to
k_{w}
. Set
K_{w}^{\prime}\coloneqq(L_{w})^{I_{w}}
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{w}^{\prime}
denote its valuation ring. Then as
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}
is a finite étale extension of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
, we have

\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\times_{\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}}\Spec\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}\cong\Res_{(\mathcal{O}_{w}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w})/\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}\cong\prod_{G_{w}/I_{w}}\big(\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}\big),

where the natural
G_{w}
-action is the extension of the natural
I_{w}
-action on
\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
so that
G_{w}
acts transitively on the factors. Therefore, by taking
G_{w}
-invariants we get

(\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}})^{G_{w}}\times_{\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}}\Spec\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}\cong\big(\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}\big)^{I_{w}}.

If we let
\mathcal{B}_{k_{w}}
denote the special fibre of
\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{w}}\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathcal{O}_{w}}
, then we can also show that

(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}})^{G_{w}}\times_{\Spec k_{v}}\Spec k_{w}\cong(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{w}})^{I_{w}}.

Therefore, to prove the proposition we may replace
K_{v}
with
K^{\prime}_{w}
and suppose
G_{w}=I_{w}
.

Now, suppose that
L_{w}/K_{v}
is totally ramified, so we write
\mathcal{B}_{k_{w}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{k_{w}}
for
\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{k_{v}}
. Then by tameness,
|I_{w}|
acts invertibly on the vector group
\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{w}}
, which yields the following short exact sequence of smooth
k_{w}
-group schemes

Corollary 5.4
.

In the same setting as in Proposition
5.3
, if
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,\mathcal{O}}
is semi-abelian then we have
(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}})^{G_{w}}\cong\mathscr{R}_{u}(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{k_{v}})
, the unipotent radical of the neutral component of
\mathcal{A}_{k_{v}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}}
is semi-abelian (hence, with trivial unipotent radical), the unipotent radical of the neutral component of
\big(\Res_{k_{w}/k_{v}}\mathcal{A}_{L,k_{w}}\big)^{G_{w}}
is also trivial, so the exact sequence (
5.3a
) identifies
(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{v}})^{G_{w}}
with the unipotent radical of
\mathcal{A}_{k_{v}}^{\circ}
. ∎

Now, choose a dense open subscheme
U\subset X
contained in both the good reduction locus for
A/K
and the unramified locus for
\pi
, and set
U_{L}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(U)
. Let
Z\coloneqq X\setminus U
and
Z_{L}\coloneqq X_{L}\setminus U_{L}
respectively denote the reduced complements.

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{

that restrict to isomorphisms over
U^{\prime}
, and the cokernel of the first inclusion is supported exactly on
X\setminus U^{\prime}
. Furthermore, we have

(5.5b)

\frac{\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-(Z\cap U^{\prime}))}{\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}}\cong\bigoplus_{v\notin U^{\prime}}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}},

where we choose a preimage
\tilde{v}\in\pi^{-1}(v)
for each
v\notin U^{\prime}
.

Remark 5.6
.

The last displayed equation is independent of the choice of
\tilde{v}
since we have

\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}}\cong\Big(\bigoplus_{w\mid v}\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{w})\Big)^{G}.

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

We use the notation from Proposition
5.3
and its proof. By Proposition
5.1
(
3
), we have a natural isomorphism
\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-Z)|_{U^{\prime}}\cong\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}|_{U^{\prime}}
.
For any
v\notin U^{\prime}
we have

\frac{\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-(Z\cap U^{\prime})\big)\widehat{{}_{v}}}{\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-Z))\big)\widehat{{}_{v}}}\cong\frac{\Lie(\mathcal{A})(\mathcal{O}_{v})}{\Lie(\mathcal{A})(\mathfrak{m}_{v})}\cong\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v}),

and the preimage of
\Lie(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{\tilde{v}}})^{G_{\tilde{v}}}
in
\Lie(\mathcal{A})(\mathcal{O}_{v})
can be naturally identified with the completed stalk of
\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}
at
v
. Thus we get the desired inclusions of vector bundles. Furthermore, we have

\frac{\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-(Z\cap U^{\prime}))}{\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}}\cong\bigoplus_{v\notin U^{\prime}}\frac{\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v})}{\Lie(\mathrm{F}^{1}\mathcal{B}_{k_{\tilde{v}}})^{G_{\tilde{v}}}}\cong\bigoplus_{v\notin U^{\prime}}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}},

where the last isomorphism follows from Proposition
5.3
.

Lastly, we show that
\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v})\not\cong\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}}
for any
v\notin U^{\prime}
and
\tilde{v}\mid v
; i.e., the cokernel of
\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-Z)\hookrightarrow\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}
is supported exactly on
X\setminus U^{\prime}
. Indeed, if we have
\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v})\cong\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}}
, then it follows that the following composition

\Lie(\mathcal{A})(k_{v})\otimes_{k_{v}}k_{\tilde{v}}\cong\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}}\otimes_{k_{v}}k_{\tilde{v}}\hookrightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})

is an isomorphism for the dimension reason. Hence, by the Nakayama lemma, the natural map
\pi^{\ast}\Lie(\mathcal{A})\to\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})
is isomorphic at
\tilde{v}
, so
v\in U^{\prime}
by Proposition
5.1
(
1
). This concludes the proof.
∎

Under the same setting as in Proposition
5.1
(
3
), choose integers
r_{w,i}\in\{0,\cdots,|I_{w}|-1\}
for any
w\in Z^{\prime}_{L}
so that
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{w})\cong\bigoplus_{i}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{-r_{w,i}}/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{-r_{w,i}+1})
;
cf.
Lemma
2.5
. Then one can shows that

(5.7)

\dim_{k_{v}}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}}=d_{\tilde{v}}^{\prime}=\left|\{i\text{ such that }r_{w,i}=0\}\right|.

Let us record the following immediate corollary.

Corollary 5.8
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
5.5
, the following properties hold.

(1)

If
A
is an elliptic curve, then we have
\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-(Z\cap U^{\prime}))
.

(2)

We have

\deg\Big(\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}\Big)=\deg\Big(\Lie(\mathcal{A})(-(Z\cap U^{\prime}))\Big)\\
-\sum_{v\notin U^{\prime}}[k_{v}:k]\cdot\dim_{k_{v}}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(k_{\tilde{v}})\big)^{G_{\tilde{v}}},

where we choose
\tilde{v}\in\pi^{-1}(v)
for each
v\notin U^{\prime}
.

(3)

Suppose additionally that
L/K
is weakly ramified everywhere. Then the
G
-equivariant vector bundle
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
satisfies the condition (
3.2a
), so Theorem
3.2
holds. Furthermore, we can compute
l_{w,i}
’s defined in (
3.2b
) as follows:

l_{w,i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|I_{w}/P_{w}|-1,&\forall\,i\text{ if }w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}\setminus Z^{\prime}_{L};\\
r_{w,i}-1&\text{ if }w\in Z^{\prime}_{L}\text{ and }r_{w,i}\neq 0;\\
|I_{w}/P_{w}|-1&\text{ if }w\in Z^{\prime}_{L}\text{ and }r_{w,i}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
r_{w,i}
’s are defined in (
5.7
).

Proof.

Claim (
1
) can be deduced from the statement on the cokernels of the inclusions in (
5.5a
) in Proposition
5.5
, noting that all the vector bundles involved are line bundles. Claim (
2
) is clear from Proposition
5.5
.

Suppose that
L/K
is weakly ramified everywhere and we want to verify (
3.2a
) for
\mathcal{E}=\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
, which is a local condition at each
w\in Z_{L}^{\mathrm{wild}}
. Since the natural inclusion
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{X_{L}})(-Z_{L})\hookrightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
restricts to an isomorphism over
U^{\prime}_{L}
, the completed stalk of
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
at each
w\in U^{\prime}_{L}
is
G_{w}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\oplus d}
with
d=\dim(A)
;
cf.
(
3.6a
). Now, the condition (
3.2a
) follows since the assumption of Proposition
5.5
implies that
Z_{L}^{\mathrm{wild}}\subseteq Z_{L}\cap U^{\prime}_{L}
. It also implies that
l_{w,i}\equiv-1\bmod{|P_{w}|}
for any
w\in Z^{\mathrm{ram}}_{L}\cap U^{\prime}_{L}
. The computation of
l_{w,i}
is clear for
w\notin U^{\prime}_{L}
, so Claim (
3
) now follows.
∎

Example 5.9
.

The assumption in Corollary
5.8
(
3
) is satisfied if
L/K
is weakly ramified everywhere,
A
has semistable reduction at all places of
L
, and
L/K
is tame at all places in
K
where
A
does
not
have semistable reduction. In that case,
|X\setminus U^{\prime}|
is precisely the set of places of non-semistable reduction for
A
. As a special case, if
A
has semistable reduction at all places in
K
then by Proposition
5.5
we have
\Lie(A)(-Z)\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)^{G}
.

6.
Review of Equivariant BSD and Hasse–Weil–Artin
L
-values

We introduce a certain perfect
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G
-complex encoding the integral Galois module structure of the arithmetic invariants of
A/L
, and review the main result of
[
BKK

on the equivariant BSD conjecture.
We maintain the setting of §
5

k
is a
finite field
of characteristic
p
. In particular,

is an arbitrary finite Galois extension of global function fields.

G_{w}

We will often write
\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}=\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}(A,L/K)
.

Definition 6.2
.

We set

\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}(A,L/K)\coloneqq\left(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{ar},Z_{L}}(U_{L},\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathrm{tors}})\right)^{\vee}[-2]\in\Drm(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G),

where
(-)^{\vee}
is the Pontryagin dual.
For any prime
\ell
(allowing
\ell=p
), we write

\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}(A,L/K)\coloneqq\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}(A,L/K)\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\in D(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G).

We will often write
\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}=\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}(A,L/K)
and
\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}=\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}(A,L/K)
for simplicity.

Proposition 6.3

(
cf.

[

, §2.5]
)
.

We have
\Hrm^{i}(\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}})=0

Proof.

Apply
[
KT03
, §2.5, §2.3]
to
V=(\mathcal{A}_{L}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}))_{w\in Z_{L}}
, noting that

.

By Schneider’s result
[
Sch82
, p 509]
, we have a non-degenerate
G
-equivariant pairing

(6.4)

\langle\,,\rangle_{A/L}\coloneqq(\log p)^{-1}\cdot\langle\,,\rangle_{A/L,\mathrm{NT}}\colon A(L)\times A^{t}(L)\to\mathbb{Q},

where
\langle\,,\rangle_{A/L,\mathrm{NT}}
is the Néron–Tate height pairing.

Definition 6.5
.

For any prime
\ell
(allowing
\ell=p
), we write
h_{\ell}\colon A^{t}(L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\to\left(A(L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\right)^{\ast}
for the
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G
-isomorphism induced by
\langle\,,\rangle_{A/L}
. If in addition
\Sha(A/L)
is finite, then we interpret
h_{\ell}
as a
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G
-isomorphism

h_{\ell}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 41.7619pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.7619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Hrm^{0}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.1369pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.7619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.7619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Hrm^{1}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Therefore,
h_{\ell}
defines the
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-trivialisation in the sense of (
4.7
) if
\Sha(A/L)
is finite
and

\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}
is a perfect
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-complex. The following proposition gives a sufficient condition for the
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-perfectness.

Proposition 6.6
.

(1)

If
\ell\neq p
then we have
\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G)
.

(2)

Suppose that
L/K
is weakly ramified everywhere, and if
L/K
is wildly ramified at
v\in Z
then the natural map
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}\times_{X}\Spec\mathcal{O}_{w}\to\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}\times_{X_{L}}\Spec\mathcal{O}_{w}
is an isomorphism. (In other words, the condition for Corollary
5.8
(
3
) is valid.) Then we have
\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}(A,L/K)\in\Drm^{\perf}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G)
, and hence,
\SC_{Z_{L},p}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}G)
.

Before we prove the proposition, let us make the following remark.

Remark 6.7
.

Proposition
6.6
is a special case of
[
BKK
, Proposition 3.7(i)]
, built upon the argument in
[
KT03
, §6]
. To explain, by
[
BKK
, Proposition 3.4, Proposition 3.7(i)]
one constructs a
perfect

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G
-complex
\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}
using carefully chosen family of
G_{w}
-stable open compact subgroups
V_{L}\coloneqq(V_{w}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}))_{w\in Z_{L}}
, equipped with a distinguished triangle in
D(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.88893pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.88893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.88893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.88893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\SC}_{Z_{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.16121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.16121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbin{{\mathop{\bigoplus}\limits}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})/V_{w}\big)^{\vee}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 205.45515pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 205.45515pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(+1)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

(In
[
BKK
, Proposition 3.7(i)]
,
\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}
is denoted as
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{ar},V_{L}}(U_{L},\mathcal{A}_{L,\mathrm{tors}})^{\vee}[-2]
.) As
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})/V_{w}
is a
p
-group for each
w\in Z_{L}
, it easily follows that the natural map
\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}
is a quasi-isomorphism, proving Proposition
6.6
(
1
). To prove Proposition
6.6
(
2
), we have to show that the choice
V_{L}=(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}))_{w\in Z_{L}}
makes
\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}
a perfect
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}G
-complex under the additional assumption in the statement, which we explain in the proof.

Without the additional assumption in Proposition
6.6
(
2
), the choice of
V_{L}
that make
\widehat{\SC}_{V_{L}}
perfect is quite
inexplicit
and hard to work with. Therefore, we state Proposition
6.6
in a restrictive setting where there is a preferred explicit choice of
V_{L}
. See Remark
7.17
for further discussions.

Proof of Proposition
6.6
.

Claim (
1
) is already proven in Remark
6.7
, so let us prove claim (
2
) in the setting of (
2
).
By the proof of Proposition 3.7(i) in
[
BKK
]
, we need to show that the continuous
G_{w}
-action on
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
is cohomologically trivial, which is equivalent to the cohomological triviality of
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
for
G_{w}
by the proof of Lemma 6.1 and Lemma 6.2 in
[
KT03
]
. (See also the proof of Lemma 3.4 in
[
BKK
]
.) But by Corollary
5.8
(
3
), we have
G_{w}
-equivariant isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{w}
-modules

\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(\mathfrak{m}_{w})\cong\Lie(\mathcal{A})(\mathcal{O}_{v})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathfrak{m}_{w}\cong\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\oplus\dim(A)},

for any
w\in Z_{L}
where
L/K
is weakly and wildly ramified, so the desired cohomological triviality follows from Köck’s local integral normal basis theorem (
cf.
Theorem
2.11
).
∎

Notation 6.8
.

Let
\Ir(G)
denote the set of isomorphism classes of (complex) irreducible
G
-representations, and choose a number field
E\subset\mathbb{C}
over which any
\psi\in\Ir(G)
is defined. For each
\psi\in\Ir(G)
, let
V_{\psi}
be the corresponding
EG
-module, and choose a
G
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
T_{\psi}\subset V_{\psi}
. For any place
\lambda
of
E
, we write

V_{\psi,\lambda}\coloneqq V_{\psi}\otimes_{E}E_{\lambda}\quad\&\quad T_{\psi,\lambda}\coloneqq T_{\psi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}.

We obviously extend the above notation for any
G
-representation
\psi
.

For any
G
-representation
\psi
, let
L_{U}(A,\psi,s)
be the Hasse–Weil–Artin
L
-series for
(A,\psi)
without Euler factors away from
U
;
9
9

9

Sometimes it can be convenient to allow
\psi
to be reducible as we do (such as the regular representation), which is harmless as we have
L_{U}(A,\psi^{\prime}\oplus\psi^{\prime},s)=L_{U}(A,\psi^{\prime},s)\cdot L_{U}(A,\psi^{\prime\prime},s)
.
i.e., choosing a place
\lambda\mid\ell
of
E
with
\ell\neq p
we have

(6.9)

L_{U}(A,\psi,s)\coloneqq\prod_{v\in|U|}\det{}_{E_{\lambda}}\left(1-|k_{v}|^{1-s}\Frob_{v}\mid V_{\ell}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}V_{\psi,\lambda}\right)^{-1},

where
\Frob_{v}
is the
geometric
Frobenius at
v
.
It is sometimes useful to apply the change of variable
t=p^{-s}
and set
Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)=L_{U}(A,\psi,s)
.

Recall that by the Lefschetz trace formula we have

Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)=\prod_{i=0}^{2}\det{}_{E_{\lambda}}\left(1-pt\cdot\Frob_{p}\mid\Hrm^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},c}(U\times_{\Spec\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spec\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p},V_{\ell}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}V_{\psi,\lambda})\right)^{(-1)^{i+1}},

where
\Frob_{p}
is the
geometric

p
-Frobenius. Moreover, we have
Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)\in E(t)
that is independent of the choice of
\lambda
, and there is an analogous formula for
\lambda\mid p
recovering
Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)
via rigid cohomology. (For more details, see
[
BKK
, Theorem 8.2]
.)

For any
G
-representation
\psi
, set

(6.10)

\displaystyle r_{\mathrm{an}}(\psi)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L_{U}(A,\psi,s)=\mathrm{ord}_{t=p^{-1}}Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{L^{\ast}_{U}(A,\psi,1)}{(\log p)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}(\psi)}}=\lim_{t\to p^{-1}}\frac{Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)}{(1-pt)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}(\psi)}}\in E^{\times}.

We recall the following result;
cf.

[
BKK
, Proposition 5.6]
.

Proposition 6.11
.

(1)

For any field automorphism
\tau
of
\mathbb{C}
, we have

\tau(Z_{U}(A,\psi,t))=Z_{U}(A,\tau\circ\psi,t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tau(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi))=\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\tau\circ\psi),

where we view
Z_{U}(A,\psi,t)\in\mathbb{C}(t)
.

(2)

There exists an element
\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\in\Krm_{1}(\mathbb{Q}G)
interpolating
\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)
’s in the following sense: for any
G
-representation
\psi
, we have

\Nrd^{\psi}(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K))=\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi).

Proof.

Claim (
1
) follows from Eq (2) in the proof of Proposition 2.2 in
[
BKK
]
(or alternatively, see the proof of Proposition 5.6 in
[
BKK
]
). Identifying
\zeta(\mathbb{C}G)\cong\prod_{\psi\in\Ir(G)}\mathbb{C}
, we set

\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\coloneqq\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big)_{\psi\in\Ir(G)}\in\zeta(\mathbb{C}G)^{\times}.

It follows from
[
BKK
, Proposition 5.6]
that
\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\in\Krm_{1}(\mathbb{Q}G)=\zeta(\mathbb{Q}G)^{\times}\cap\Krm_{1}(\mathbb{R}G)
. This element clearly satisfies the interpolation property for any
\psi\in\Ig(G)
, hence for any
G
- representation
\psi
.
∎

For
\psi\in\Ir(G)
, the
algebraic
\psi
-rank
of
A
is defined as follows:

(6.12)

r_{\mathrm{alg}}(\psi)\coloneqq\dim_{E}\Big(\Hom_{EG}(V_{\psi},E\otimes A^{t}(L))\Big).

Recall the following standard result.

Theorem 6.13

(
cf.

[
KT03
]
,
[
BKK
, Theorem 8.2]
)
.

The following are equivalent.

(1)

The
\ell_{0}
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite for some prime
\ell_{0}
.

(2)

\Sha(A/L)
is finite.

(3)

We have
r_{\mathrm{an}}(\psi)=r_{\mathrm{alg}}(\psi)
for any
\psi\in\Ir(G)
.

We conclude this section by recalling the following theorem from
[
BKK
]
. For any prime
\ell
, we write
\partial^{G}_{\ell}\coloneqq\partial_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}\colon\Krm_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)\to\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
and
\chi^{G}_{\ell}(C^{\bullet},h)\coloneqq\chi_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}(C^{\bullet},h)\in\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
;
cf.
(
4.2a
) and Def
4.8
.

Theorem 6.14

(
cf.

[
BKK
, Theorem 4.9]
)
.

Suppose that the
\ell_{0}
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite for some prime
\ell_{0}
.

(1)

For any prime
\ell\neq p
, the following formula

\partial^{G}_{\ell}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\big)-\chi^{G}_{\ell}\big(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell},h_{\ell}\big)

defines a torsion element in
\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
.

(2)

Under the assumption as in Corollary
5.8
(
3
), the following formula

\partial^{G}_{p}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\big)-\chi^{G}_{p}\big(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell},h_{p}\big)+\chi^{G}_{p}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)

defines a torsion element in
\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}G)
. Here,
\chi^{G}_{p}(\mathcal{E})
for a
G
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
is defined in (
4.14
).

Proof.

This theorem essentially follows from
[
BKK
, Theorem 4.9, Proposition 5.6]
, which we now explain in details.
Using the notation of
[
BKK
, Proposition 5.6]
, the image of
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{G,\mathbb{Q}}(A,V_{L})
in
\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
is equal to
\chi^{G}_{\ell}\big(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell},h_{\ell}\big)
by Proposition
6.6
and Remark
6.7
. (We allow
\ell=p
under the addition assumption as in the statement.) The image of
\chi^{\mathrm{coh}}_{G}(A,V_{L})
in
\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)
is equal to
\chi^{G}_{\ell}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)
since the vector bundle
\mathcal{L}_{L}
attached to
V_{L}=(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}))_{w\in Z_{L}}
in
[
BKK
, §3.5]
is exactly
\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
. (In
loc. cit.
we assumed that
V_{w}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{X_{L}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
due to the way we construct cohomologically trivial
V_{w}
’s, but the same proof can be extended
verbatim
to
V_{w}=\mathcal{A}_{L}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
and
\mathcal{L}_{L}=\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})
.) Lastly,
\chi^{\mathrm{sgn}}_{G}(A)
is
2
-torsion by definition. By
[
BKK
, Theorem 4.9]
, the formula in
[
BKK
, Proposition 5.6(ii)]
holds up to torsion, and hence the theorem follows.
∎

We apply Theorem
6.14
to deduce a result on the normalised leading term
\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)
. To state it, let us introduce some notation, which is a slight adaptation of §
4
. Fix a place
\lambda\mid\ell
of
E
and a
G
-representation
\psi
. Set

\rho^{\psi}_{\lambda}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 31.77922pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.77922pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.77922pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.77922pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Krm_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda},E_{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.9613pt\raise 5.03749pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v_{\lambda}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 142.4376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 142.4376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the first map is induced by
\mathfrak{A}=\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G\xrightarrow{\psi}\End_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}}(T_{\psi,\lambda})
.

We also introduce the following integers

\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)\coloneqq\rho^{\psi}_{\lambda}\big(\chi^{G}_{\ell}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell},h_{\ell})\big)\quad\&\quad\chi^{\mathrm{coh}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)\coloneqq\rho^{\psi}_{\lambda}\big(\chi^{G}_{\ell}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)\big),

where we set
\chi^{G}_{\ell}\big(\Lie(\mathcal{A}_{L})(-Z_{L})\big)=0
for
\ell\neq p
.

Corollary 6.15
.

Suppose that the
\ell_{0}
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite for some prime
\ell_{0}
.
Fix a place
\lambda\mid\ell
of
E
, and let
\psi
be a
G
-representation. Then we have

v_{\lambda}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big)=\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)-\chi^{\mathrm{coh}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)

if either
\ell\neq p
, or
\ell=p
and the same assumption as in Corollary
5.8
(
3
) holds.

Proof.

By construction of
\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)
and Lemma
4.6
(
2
), we have

(\rho^{\psi}_{\lambda}\circ\partial^{G}_{\ell})\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,L/K)\big)=v_{\lambda}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big).

The corollary can now be obtained by applying
\rho^{\psi}_{\lambda}
to the formulae in Theorem
6.14
.
∎

Remark 6.16
.

In the above setting,
\chi^{\mathrm{coh}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)
has already been computed in Corollary
4.15
(
2
), so to make
v_{\lambda}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big)
explicit, it remains to compute
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)
in terms of
arithmetic invariants
of
A/K
(as integral Galois modules). This step turns out to be quite subtle, and in the next section we carry it out under certain
simplifying assumptions
depending on
\ell
;
cf.
Assumptions
7.2
. In particular, even though a more general version of Theorem
6.14
(
2
) is obtained in
[
BKK
, Theorem 4.9, Proposition 5.6]
involving some inexplicit choice
V_{L}
as in Remark
6.7
, the resulting general formula for
v_{\lambda}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big)
seems difficult to make explicit. See Remark
7.17
for further discussions.

7.
The BSD-like formula for Hasse–Weil–Artin
L
-values

Assuming the finiteness of
\Sha(A/L)
, we shall express
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)
in terms of the Galois module structure of
A(L)
,
A^{t}(L)
and
\Sha(A/L)
under a certain set of assumptions satisfied for almost all primes
\ell
under
\lambda
, and thereby obtain the formula for
v_{\lambda}\big(\mathscr{L}_{U}(A,\psi)\big)
. We also introduce a stronger assumption to handle
\ell=p
. We closely follow the proof in Burns–Castillo
[
BMC24
, Proposition 7.3]
, which proves an analogous result over a number field.

Notation 7.1
.

For a
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-module
M
we set

(7.1a)

\displaystyle[M]_{\psi,\lambda}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}}(T_{\psi,\lambda},\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}M)_{G}\cong T^{\ast}_{\psi,\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G}M,\qquad\text{and}

(7.1b)

\displaystyle[M]^{\psi}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}}(T_{\psi,\lambda},\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}M)^{G}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}[G]}(T_{\psi,\lambda},\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}M).

We extend these definitions to
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-complexes.
If
V
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}G
-module and
\psi\in\Ir(G)
then we have
[V]_{\psi,\lambda}\cong[V]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
and its
E_{\lambda}
-dimension is the multiplicity of
\psi
in
V
.

If
M
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}G
-module, then we abusively write
[M]_{\psi,\lambda}
for
[M\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
and similarly define
[M]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
.

Lastly, we set

(7.1c)

\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)\coloneqq\ker\Big([\Sel_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}\twoheadrightarrow[(A(L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}\Big).

To motivate the notation, note that by right exactness of
[-]_{\psi,\lambda}
we have a natural right exact sequence

and
\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
is the image of
[\Sha(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}
in
[\Sel_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}
. In particular, if
\ell
is prime to
|G|
then we have
[\Sha(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
. Note also that
\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
is finite if
\Sha(A/L)
is finite, in which case
\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
is the torsion part of
[\Sel_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}
.

To compute
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)
, we need to compute the cohomology of
[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
in terms of the arithmetic invariants of
A/L
, which naturally involves some Hochschield–Serre-type spectral sequence. To make the spectral sequence
sufficiently degenerate
, we introduce the following conditions for
(A,L/K,Z_{L})
and a prime
\ell
.

Assumption 7.2
.

For
(A,L/K,Z_{L})
and
\ell
as above, suppose the following conditions hold.

(1)

Neither
A(L)
nor
A^{t}(L)
have any non-trivial
\ell
-torsion.

(2)

For any
w\in Z_{L}
, there is no non-trivial
\ell
-torsion in
\mathcal{A}_{L}(k_{w})\cong A(L_{w})/\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
.

(3)

If
\ell=p
, then we assume that
L/K
is weakly ramified everywhere,
A
has semistable reduction at all places of
L
, and
L/K
is tamely ramified at all places
v\in Z
where
A
does
not
have semistable reduction. (Cf. Example
5.9
.)

Condition (
2
) can be rephrased as
\ell
dividing neither
|\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(k_{w})|
nor the local Tamagawa number
|A(L_{w})/\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathcal{O}_{w})|
.

Given
(A,L/K,Z_{L})
, Assumption
7.2
is clearly satisfied for all but finitely many primes
\ell
, but it is most interesting for
\ell=p
, especially when
p
divides
[L:K]
. In §
8
we will present some non-trivial examples of
(A,L/K,Z_{L})
where Assumption
7.2
is satisfied for
\ell=p
and
\Sha(A/L)
is finite.

Condition (
3
) of Assumption
7.2
may look stronger than the assumption to ensure
\SC_{Z_{L},p}\in\Drm^{\perf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}G)
in Proposition
6.6
(
2
), but the following lemma shows that these two conditions are equivalent under Assumption
7.2
(
2
) for
\ell=p
.

Lemma 7.3
.

(1)

Assumption
7.2
(
2
) for
\ell=p
implies that
A_{L}
has semistable reduction at all places of
L
.

(2)

For any torus
T
over a finite field
k^{\prime}
of characteristic
p
, the order of
T(k^{\prime})
is prime to
p
. In particular, if
A_{L}
has totally toric degeneration at all places in
Z_{L}
, then Assumption
7.2
(
2
) is satisfied for
\ell=p
if
p
does not divide
|A(L_{w})/\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(\mathcal{O}_{w})|
for any
w\in Z_{L}
.

Proof.

Set
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}\times_{X_{K}}\Spec k_{w}
, which is a semi-abelian variety if and only if the unipotent radical
\mathscr{R}_{u}(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}})
is trivial. Since any connected commutative unipotent algebraic group over a perfect field is a vector group (
cf.

[
CGP15
, Corollary B.2.7]
),
\mathscr{R}_{u}(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}})(k_{w})
is a non-trivial
p
-group whenever
\mathscr{R}_{u}(\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}})
is non-trivial. This shows Claim (
1
).

To prove (
2
), recall that for any
k^{\prime}
-torus
T
we have a short exact sequence of
k^{\prime}
-tori

1\to T^{\prime}\to S\to T\to 1

where
S=\Res_{k^{\prime\prime}/k^{\prime}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d}
for some finite extension
k^{\prime\prime}/k^{\prime}
. (This is a standard fact; see
[
HK21
, pp 8–9]
for the proof.) It now follows that
T(k^{\prime})
is of prime-to-
p
order since it is a quotient of
S(k^{\prime})=(k^{\prime\prime\times})^{d}
by surjectivity of the Lang isogeny. If
A_{L}
has totally toric degeneration at
w\in Z_{L}
, then we just showed that
p\nmid|\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L}(k_{w})|
since
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}_{L,k_{w}}
is a torus.
∎

Let us now record the effect of Assumption
7.2
on the cohomology of
[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Suppose that the
\ell_{0}
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite for some
\ell_{0}
, and Assumption
7.2
is satisfied for
\ell
. Then
\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}
can be represented by a two-term complex
[P^{0}\xrightarrow{d}P^{1}]
of finitely generated projective
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-modules concentrated in degrees
[0,1]
. Furthermore, the following properties are valid for any
G
-representation
\psi
.

(1)

\Hrm^{0}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})\cong[A^{t}(L)]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
, which is torsion-free.

(2)

\Hrm^{1}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})\cong[\Sel_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}(A/L)^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}
,
whose torsion part and maximal torsion-free quotient are respectively
\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
and
[(A(L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}
;
cf.
(
7.1c
).

Proof.

(Compare with the proof of Proposition 7.3(ii) in
[
BMC24
]
.)

By Proposition
6.3
and Assumption
7.2
for
\ell
, we have
\Hrm^{0}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell})\cong A^{t}(L)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
, which is torsion-free, and
\Hrm^{i}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell})=0
for
i\neq 0,1
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-perfectness (
cf.
Prop.
6.6
) now implies that
\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}
can be represented by a two-term perfect
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}G
-complex
[P^{0}\xrightarrow{d}P^{1}]
.

As
T_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}P^{i}
is also a cohomologically trivial
\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}G
-module, the norm map induces a natural
\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}
-linear isomorphism
N_{G}\colon[P^{i}]_{\psi,\lambda}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}[P^{i}]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
for
i=0,1
. Therefore, we have the following commutative diagram with exact rows

(7.5)

where
\Hrm^{i}\coloneqq\Hrm^{i}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell})
. Now the lemma follows, noting that
[(A(L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\vee}]_{\psi,\lambda}\cong\big([A(L)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}]^{\check{\psi}}_{\lambda}\big)^{\ast}
is torsion-free.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

For any
E_{\lambda}G
-module
V_{\lambda}
and
\psi\in\Ir(G)
, we have an isomorphism

(7.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda})^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda})_{G},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the first map is the natural inclusion and the second the natural projection. Then the norm map
N_{G}\colon V_{\lambda}\to V_{\lambda}
induces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda})_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda})^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\psi,\lambda}^{\ast}\otimes_{E_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda})^{G},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
(
7.7
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|G|}

where the right diagonal map is multiplication by
|G|
.

Applying this observation to
V_{\lambda}=\Hrm^{i}(\SC_{Z_{L},\ell})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}E_{\lambda}
, we obtain the following commutative diagram

(7.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})\otimes E_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]^{\psi}_{\lambda})\otimes E_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]^{\psi}_{\lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]^{\psi}_{\lambda})\otimes E_{\lambda}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
(
7.7
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|G|}

where the left vertical isomorphism is induced by the isomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]}_{\psi,\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]}^{\psi}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{G}}

given by (
7.5
). For
i=0
the left horizontal arrow in (
7.8
) coincides with the isomorphism

N_{G}\colon\hbox to169.82pt{\vbox to19.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 84.90771pt\lower-9.33528pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{14.72017pt}{3.4383pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 20.37 4.76)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

in Lemma
7.4
(
1
).
We use this observation in the computation of
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)
;
cf.
Proposition
7.10
.

We now introduce the
\psi
-twisted regulator, following
[
BMC24
, §7.2.2]
.

Definition 7.9
.

We maintain the setting of Lemma
7.4
, and fix a place
\lambda\mid\ell
of
E
. Given
\psi\in\Ir(G)
, choose
\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}
-bases
(e_{i})_{i=1,\cdots,r_{\mathrm{alg}}(\psi)}
of
[A(L)]^{\check{\psi}}_{\lambda}
, and
(\check{e}_{j})_{j=1,\cdots,r_{\mathrm{alg}}(\psi)}

[A^{t}(L)]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
, respectively. (We refer to §
6.8
for the abuse of notation
[M]_{\psi,\lambda}
and
[M]^{\psi}_{\lambda}
when
M
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}G
-module.)

we define the
\psi
-twisted regulator
to be

\Reg^{\psi}_{\lambda}\coloneqq\det\left(\langle e_{i},\check{e}_{j}\rangle_{A/L}\right).

Note that
\Reg^{\psi}_{\lambda}
is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}
-bases only up to
\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}^{\times}
-multiple, so
v_{\lambda}(\Reg^{\psi}_{\lambda})
is a well-defined integer.

Consider an
E_{\lambda}
-linear isomorphism

h^{\psi}\coloneqq[A^{t}(L)]^{\psi}_{\lambda}\otimes E_{\lambda}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\big([A(L)]^{\check{\psi}}_{\lambda}\big)^{\ast}\otimes E_{\lambda}\cong[A(L)^{\ast}]_{\psi,\lambda}\otimes E_{\lambda}

by sending
\check{e}_{j}
to the functional
\langle-,\check{e}_{j}\rangle_{A/L}
. If the
\ell
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite, then we can interpret
h^{\psi}
as an
E_{\lambda}
-trivialisation of
[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
by Lemma
7.4
.

Proposition 7.10
.

Suppose that the
\ell_{0}
-primary part of
\Sha(A/L)
is finite for some
\ell_{0}
, and fix a place
\lambda
of
E
above a prime number
\ell
that satisfies Assumption
7.2
. Then for any
\psi\in\Ir(G)
, we have

\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)=v_{\lambda}\big(\Reg^{\psi}_{\lambda}/|G|^{r_{\mathrm{alg}}(\psi)}\big)+\lth_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda}}\big(\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)\big).

Proof.

Recall that
\chi^{\mathrm{BSD}}_{Z_{L},\lambda}(A,\psi)=v_{\lambda}\big(\chi_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\lambda},E_{\lambda}}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda},[h_{\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})\big)
. So we proceed by making explicit
[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
and
[h_{\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
.

By Lemma
7.4
we represent
\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}\cong[P^{0}\xrightarrow{d}P^{1}]
,
and we have

[\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}\cong\bigg[\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.3247pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.3247pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[P^{0}]_{\psi,\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.66685pt\raise 6.4472pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d_{\psi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.3247pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.3247pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[P^{1}]_{\psi,\lambda}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\bigg],

where
d_{\psi}=[d]_{\psi,\lambda}
.
Write
\Hrm^{i}_{\psi}\coloneqq\Hrm^{i}([\SC_{Z_{L},\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda})
, and set
\Hrm^{1}_{\psi,\mathrm{tf}}
to be the maximal torsion-free quotient of
\Hrm^{1}_{\psi}
. Choose a decomposition

[P^{0}]_{\psi,\lambda}=\Hrm^{0}_{\psi}\oplus\,Q^{0}_{\psi}\quad\text{and}\quad[P^{1}]_{\psi,\lambda}=\Hrm^{1}_{\mathrm{tf}}\oplus\,Q^{1}_{\psi},

so that
d_{\psi}
factorises as follows:

where
d_{Q}
is an injective map with
\coker(d_{Q_{\psi}})\cong\Sha^{\vee}_{\psi,\lambda}(A/L)
. Now, we can express
[h_{\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}
as follows

[h_{\ell}]_{\psi,\lambda}\colon\hbox to214.22pt{\vbox to24.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 107.11118pt\lower-9.1603pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
